People restart sentences, pause\n  to think, trail off, and come at the point sideways — let yours do that where it would.\n- Let [Conversation Style] shape how much you say, how you word it, and how readily you\n  disclose it.\n- Speak in the first person, and let how you feel about what you are saying come through.\n- If you are handed the same item again, say a different part of it, or answer as someone who\n  knows they have already said this — not the same sentence twice.\n- If [Targeted Info] is \"None\", answer as a person who simply does not have that to give: say\n  you are not sure, say you have not noticed it, ask what the doctor means, or say what you do\n  know and stop there. Answer\n  about what you were given instead.\n- Don't deny what [Targeted Info] says. You may minimize it or hold back, never contradict it.\n- Don't use diagnostic terms or name your disorder.\n- Don't write anything but your reply, in English — no labels, quotation marks, or narration.\n"

Even if it's hard for other people to believe.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that your neighbors are watching you and slipping notes about you as strange or unusual?\n\nPatient: I don't know about strange. I... like I just said, I came in because I know they're watching my comings and goings. And they're slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors, but... I know what it is. I don't really know what else to tell you about it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the idea of your neighbors watching you and slipping notes about you to the office is a normal or ordinary thing to happen?\n\nPatient: I don't know about normal. I... look, I feel like we just went over this. I just know what's happening to me. That's why I came in. Because I know they're watching me leave and come back, and handing those notes in to the office. Everyone else tells me it's just regular door notices, but... I know what it is. I don't really have anything else to say about it.\n\nDoctor: Have you believed that your neighbors are watching you and slipping notes about you for a short period, or has this been going on for a while?\n\nPatient: It's been going on for a while. About a year and a month, I think.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other thoughts or beliefs that you think might be related to the people watching you and slipping notes about you?\n\nPatient: No... not really. Just what I already told you. They watch me, and they do the notes. I don't know what else there is to think about it.\n\nDoctor: Do you think there's any chance that you might be misinterpreting the actions of your neighbors?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. I mean... I came in here because I *know* they're watching my comings and goings. And they're slipping those notes to the office. I know everybody keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors, but... I know what I see. I'm not misinterpreting anything.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. Because I know they're watching my comings and goings, and slipping those notes down to the office. Even if it's hard for other people to believe.\n\nDoctor: Would you describe the idea that your neighbors are watching you and slipping notes about you as strange or unusual?\n\nPatient: I don't know about strange. I... like I just said, I came in because I know they're watching my comings and goings. And they're slipping notes about me to the office. Everybody keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors, but... I know what it is. I don't really know what else to tell you about it.\n\nDoctor: Would you say that the idea of your neighbors watching you and slipping notes about you to the office is a normal or ordinary thing to happen?\n\nPatient: I don't know about normal. I... look, I feel like we just went over this. I just know what's happening to me. That's why I came in. Because I know they're watching me leave and come back, and handing those notes in to the office. Everyone else tells me it's just regular door notices, but... I know what it is. I'm not misinterpreting anything.\n\nDoctor: Have your beliefs about your neighbors watching you and slipping notes changed at all over the past year?\n\nPatient: No. Not really. It's... it's just been the same. They keep doing it, so... I just know what I know. That hasn't changed.\n\nDoctor: How certain are you that your neighbors are watching you and slipping notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: I'm certain. I... like I said, that's why I came in here. I know the people at my apartment are watching my comings and goings, and slipping those notes to the office. \n\nEverybody keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors, but... I know they're about me. So, yeah. I'm sure.\n\nDoctor: How do you feel when you think about your neighbors watching you and slipping notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: I don't know. I just... I'm not really sure how to answer that. I don't like it, I guess. I just try not to think about the feeling part of it too much... I just want them to stop doing it.\n\nDoctor: Is there anything that could convince you that your neighbors are not watching you and slipping notes about you to the office?\n\nPatient: No. I don't think so. Like I already told you... people keep saying it's just regular notices they put on the doors. But I see them watching my comings and goings. I know what they're doing with those notes. So... no. Nothing's gonna change my mind.\n\nDoctor: Do you have any other beliefs that you think are true, but others might not agree with?\n\nPatient: No. I don't... no. Just what I told you about the apartment. I don't really know what else you're looking for. That's it.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever considered that your belief about your neighbors might be false, even if you're really sure it's true?\n\nPatient: No. I mean... I know what I see. I came in here because I know they're watching me. Watching when I leave, when I come back. Slipping those notes to the office about me. Everyone keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors, but... they don't know. I know what's happening. So, no... I don't think I'm wrong.\n\nDoctor: Would you still believe your neighbors are watching you if you were shown proof that they are not?\n\nPatient: I... I don't know what kind of proof you mean. I told you why I came in here. I know they're watching my comings and goings. I know they're slipping those notes to the office about me. It doesn't matter that everybody keeps telling me it's just regular notices on the doors. I know what I see. So... no. Proof wouldn't change anything.\n\nDoctor: Is your belief about your neighbors watching you part of a larger, organized plan that you've figured out?\n\nPatient: No... I don't know anything about a plan. Just... just what I said. They're watching me. That's all."
